### Question 48: Proximal Tubular Formate and Oxalate Transport: The Substrate Shuttles
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the apical membrane transport mechanisms in the proximal tubule, specifically the role of the CFEX (SLC26A6) transporter in the formate/oxalate shuttle for NaCl reabsorption and oxalate secretion. (考察近端小管顶膜转运机制，特别是CFEX (SLC26A6)转运蛋白在甲酸/草酸穿梭中的作用，用于NaCl重吸收和草酸分泌。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the nephrology clinic with recurrent episodes of severe flank pain and hematuria since childhood. His past medical history is significant for multiple calcium oxalate kidney stones, diagnosed via CT scan. Laboratory investigations reveal normal serum electrolytes (Na+ 140 mEq/L, K+ 4.2 mEq/L, Cl- 102 mEq/L, HCO3- 24 mEq/L), normal renal function (BUN 15 mg/dL, Cr 0.8 mg/dL), and normal serum calcium (10.0 mg/dL) and phosphate (3.5 mg/dL). A 24-hour urine collection shows elevated oxalate excretion (120 mg/day, normal <40 mg/day) and normal citrate excretion. Genetic testing reveals a homozygous loss-of-function mutation in the *SLC26A6* gene. Cellular transport studies in proximal tubule cells from this patient demonstrate severely impaired apical Cl-/HCO3- exchange and reduced transcellular oxalate secretion. The mechanism underlying the apical membrane transport of small organic monocarboxylates, including formate, involves an exchanger that facilitates the movement of these molecules across the apical membrane. This exchanger is crucial for both driving NaCl reabsorption and secreting oxalate. The intracellular recycling of the counter-ion involved in this process is essential for maintaining the transport activity. Which small organic monocarboxylate acts as an intracellular recycling counter-ion to drive transcellular NaCl reabsorption across the apical membrane via the CFEX transporter?

(A) Oxalate
(B) Citrate
(C) Acetate
(D) Formate
(E) Lactate

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** Young male with recurrent calcium oxalate kidney stones since childhood. This points towards a metabolic abnormality affecting oxalate handling.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Normal electrolytes, renal function, calcium, and phosphate rule out primary disorders of these parameters. Elevated 24-hour urine oxalate confirms hyperoxaluria as the cause of stone formation. Normal citrate excretion rules out hypocitraturia as the primary cause (though it can coexist).
- **Genetic Testing:** Homozygous loss-of-function mutation in *SLC26A6*. This gene encodes the CFEX (Cl-/HCO3- exchanger) transporter, located on the apical membrane of proximal tubule cells.
- **Cellular Transport Studies:** Impaired apical Cl-/HCO3- exchange and reduced transcellular oxalate secretion confirm the functional defect due to the *SLC26A6* mutation.
- **Question Focus:** The question asks about the specific small organic monocarboxylate that acts as an intracellular recycling counter-ion for the CFEX transporter in driving NaCl reabsorption.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Nephron Segment Biophysics (肾脏节段转运机制与生物物理推导)
- **Nephron Segment:** Proximal Convoluted Tubule (PCT), specifically the apical membrane (luminal side).
- **Transporter:** CFEX (SLC26A6), an anion exchanger located on the apical membrane of the PCT.
- **Primary Function:** CFEX mediates two key processes:
    1.  **NaCl Reabsorption:** CFEX exchanges intracellular HCO3- for luminal Cl-. This process is coupled with the apical Na+/H+ exchanger (NHE3), which brings H+ into the cell in exchange for luminal Na+. The intracellular H+ combines with HCO3- to form H2CO3, which is rapidly broken down by carbonic anhydrase (CA) into CO2 and H2O. CO2 diffuses into the lumen.
    2.  **Oxalate Secretion:** CFEX also mediates the active secretion of metabolic oxalate from the blood into the tubular lumen.
- **Formate Shuttle Mechanism:** The key to sustained NaCl reabsorption via CFEX lies in the recycling of the intracellular HCO3- counter-ion.
    1.  Luminal Cl- enters the cell via CFEX in exchange for intracellular HCO3-.
    2.  The intracellular HCO3- combines with H+ (from NHE3 activity) to form H2CO3.
    3.  H2CO3 dissociates into CO2 and H2O.
    4.  CO2 diffuses into the lumen.
    5.  In the acidic luminal environment (pH ~6.5-7.0), CO2 reacts with H2O to form H2CO3, which then dissociates into H+ and HCO3-.
    6.  The neutral molecule, formic acid (HCOOH), is formed from the protonation of formate (HCOO-).
    7.  Formic acid (HCOOH) is lipid-soluble and diffuses back into the cell.
    8.  Inside the cell, formic acid dissociates into H+ and formate (HCOO-).
    9.  The regenerated formate (HCOO-) is then available to be exchanged for luminal Cl- via CFEX, completing the cycle.
- **Role of Formate:** Formate (HCOO-) acts as the intracellular recycling counter-ion. It is generated in the lumen from CO2, protonated to formic acid (HCOOH), diffuses into the cell, and then dissociates back into formate, ready for the next exchange cycle with Cl-. This recycling mechanism allows the CFEX transporter to continuously facilitate NaCl reabsorption without depleting intracellular HCO3-.
- **Role of Oxalate:** Oxalate is also transported by CFEX, but it does not participate in the recycling counter-ion mechanism for NaCl reabsorption. Its secretion is an independent function of the transporter.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1.  The patient has hyperoxaluria and calcium oxalate stones, linked to a mutation in *SLC26A6*.
2.  *SLC26A6* encodes the CFEX transporter in the PCT apical membrane.
3.  CFEX is involved in both Cl-/HCO3- exchange (for NaCl reabsorption) and oxalate secretion.
4.  The question asks about the counter-ion recycled intracellularly to drive NaCl reabsorption via CFEX.
5.  The mechanism involves the exchange of intracellular HCO3- for luminal Cl-.
6.  To sustain this exchange, the intracellular HCO3- must be regenerated.
7.  CO2 diffuses into the lumen, forms H2CO3, then H+ and HCO3-.
8.  Formic acid (HCOOH), derived from formate (HCOO-), diffuses into the cell.
9.  Inside the cell, formic acid dissociates into H+ and formate (HCOO-).
10. The regenerated formate (HCOO-) is the counter-ion recycled for the next Cl-/HCO3- exchange.
11. Therefore, formate is the small organic monocarboxylate acting as the intracellular recycling counter-ion.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) Oxalate:** Oxalate is also transported by CFEX, but it is secreted into the lumen, not recycled intracellularly to drive NaCl reabsorption. It is the substrate being secreted, not the counter-ion for NaCl reabsorption. Incorrect.
- **(B) Citrate:** Citrate is primarily reabsorbed in the PCT via Na+-dependent cotransporters (e.g., SLC22A3) and is involved in inhibiting calcium stone formation, but it is not directly involved in the CFEX-mediated formate shuttle for NaCl reabsorption. Incorrect.
- **(C) Acetate:** Acetate is a small organic acid, but it is not the specific counter-ion involved in the CFEX-mediated formate shuttle mechanism for NaCl reabsorption in the PCT. Incorrect.
- **(D) Formate:** Formate (HCOO-) is the correct answer. As detailed in the physiological mechanism, it is generated in the lumen, protonated to formic acid (HCOOH), diffuses into the cell, and dissociates back to formate, which is then exchanged for Cl- via CFEX, thus recycling the counter-ion necessary for continuous NaCl reabsorption. Correct.
- **(E) Lactate:** Lactate is another small organic acid, but like acetate, it is not the specific counter-ion involved in the CFEX-mediated formate shuttle mechanism for NaCl reabsorption. Incorrect.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)
| Feature             | CFEX (SLC26A6) Transport | Formate Shuttle Role                                  |
| :------------------ | :----------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------- |
| Location            | PCT Apical Membrane      | Intracellular Recycling                               |
| Primary Substrates  | Cl-/HCO3-, Oxalate       | Formate (HCOO-) / Formic Acid (HCOOH)                 |
| Process             | Anion Exchange           | Counter-ion Recycling for NaCl Reabsorption          |
| Mechanism           | HCO3- (in) <-> Cl- (out)  | CO2 -> H2CO3 -> H+ + HCO3- -> HCOOH (diffuses in) -> H+ + HCOO- (recycled) |
| Clinical Relevance  | Hyperoxaluria, Stones   | Essential for sustained PCT NaCl reabsorption        |
| Genetic Defect      | *SLC26A6* mutations      | Impaired NaCl reabsorption & Oxalate secretion        |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
The CFEX (SLC26A6) transporter in the proximal tubule apical membrane mediates both Cl-/HCO3- exchange (driving NaCl reabsorption) and oxalate secretion. The formate shuttle mechanism is crucial for sustaining NaCl reabsorption via CFEX, where CO2-derived formate (HCOO-) is protonated to formic acid (HCOOH) in the lumen, diffuses into the cell, and is regenerated as formate, acting as the recycled counter-ion for Cl- exchange. Defects in CFEX lead to hyperoxaluria and calcium oxalate kidney stones.

### Question 49: Quantitative Calculation: Calculating Filtered Load and Excretion of Solutes in S1
- **Difficulty**: Easy
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Calculating filtered load (过滤负荷) of a solute based on glomerular filtration rate (GFR) and plasma concentration (血浆浓度).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male research volunteer is participating in a study investigating renal tubular handling of electrolytes. His baseline renal function is normal. During the study, his glomerular filtration rate (GFR) is measured using inulin clearance and found to be 120 mL/min. A blood sample drawn simultaneously reveals a plasma bicarbonate (HCO3-) concentration of 25 mEq/L. The research protocol requires calculating the amount of bicarbonate filtered into Bowman's space per minute.

What is the filtered load of bicarbonate in this patient, expressed in mEq/min?

(A) 1.5 mEq/min
(B) 2.0 mEq/min
(C) 2.4 mEq/min
(D) 3.0 mEq/min
(E) 4.0 mEq/min

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient:** 35-year-old male research volunteer with normal baseline renal function.
- **Key Measurement:** Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) = 120 mL/min. This represents the volume of plasma filtered across the glomerular capillaries into Bowman's space per unit time.
- **Key Laboratory Value:** Plasma bicarbonate (HCO3-) concentration = 25 mEq/L. This represents the concentration of bicarbonate in the plasma that is available to be filtered.
- **Question:** Calculate the filtered load of bicarbonate per minute. Filtered load is the total amount of a substance filtered per unit time.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Nephron Segment Biophysics (肾脏节段转运机制与生物物理推导)
- **Filtered Load Calculation:** The fundamental principle is that the filtered load of any substance is the product of its concentration in the plasma entering the glomerulus and the GFR.
- **Formula:** Filtered Load (FL) = GFR × Plasma Concentration (P).
- **Units:** GFR is in volume/time (mL/min or L/min). Plasma concentration is in amount/volume (mEq/L or mg/dL). The resulting filtered load is in amount/time (mEq/min or mg/min).
- **Application to Bicarbonate:** In this case, we need to calculate the filtered load of bicarbonate (FL_HCO3).
    - GFR = 120 mL/min
    - Plasma HCO3- concentration (P_HCO3) = 25 mEq/L
    - FL_HCO3 = GFR × P_HCO3 = (120 mL/min) × (25 mEq/L)
    - **Unit Conversion:** Since 1 L = 1000 mL, we can convert mL to L: 120 mL/min = 0.12 L/min.
    - FL_HCO3 = (0.12 L/min) × (25 mEq/L) = 3.0 mEq/min.

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. Identify the required calculation: Filtered load of bicarbonate.
2. Identify the necessary inputs: GFR and plasma bicarbonate concentration.
3. Recall the formula for filtered load: Filtered Load = GFR × Plasma Concentration.
4. Substitute the given values into the formula: Filtered Load = 120 mL/min × 25 mEq/L.
5. Perform the calculation, ensuring correct unit conversion (mL to L): Filtered Load = 0.12 L/min × 25 mEq/L = 3.0 mEq/min.
6. Compare the calculated result with the given options.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) 1.5 mEq/min:** This value is half of the correct answer (3.0 mEq/min). It might result from incorrectly using GFR = 60 mL/min or plasma HCO3- = 12.5 mEq/L, or incorrectly calculating (120 mL/min * 25 mEq/L) / 2.
- **(B) 2.0 mEq/min:** This value is incorrect. It might result from incorrectly using GFR = 80 mL/min or plasma HCO3- = 16.7 mEq/L, or incorrectly calculating (120 mL/min * 25 mEq/L) / 1.5.
- **(C) 2.4 mEq/min:** This value is incorrect. It might result from incorrectly using GFR = 96 mL/min or plasma HCO3- = 20 mEq/L, or incorrectly calculating (120 mL/min * 25 mEq/L) / 1.25.
- **(D) 3.0 mEq/min:** This is the correct calculation: (120 mL/min) × (25 mEq/L) = 3000 mEq·mL/min. Converting mL to L: 3000 mEq·mL/min / 1000 mL/L = 3.0 mEq/min.
- **(E) 4.0 mEq/min:** This value is higher than the correct answer. It might result from incorrectly using GFR = 160 mL/min or plasma HCO3- = 33.3 mEq/L, or incorrectly calculating (120 mL/min * 25 mEq/L) * 1.33.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)
| Parameter          | Value          | Unit         | Formula                     |
|-------------------|----------------|--------------|-----------------------------|
| GFR                | 120            | mL/min       |                             |
| Plasma HCO3-       | 25             | mEq/L        |                             |
| Filtered Load (FL) | Calculation    | mEq/min      | FL = GFR × Plasma Conc.     |
| Calculation        | 120 * 25       |              | (0.12 L/min) * (25 mEq/L)   |
| Result             | 3.0            | mEq/min      |                             |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
Calculating filtered load is a fundamental concept in renal physiology. It represents the total amount of a substance presented to the renal tubules per unit time. It is calculated by multiplying the GFR by the plasma concentration of the substance. This value is crucial for understanding tubular reabsorption and secretion processes, as it represents the starting point for the tubules' handling of that substance. Understanding this calculation is essential for interpreting renal function tests and understanding various renal pathologies.

### Question 50: Proximal Tubule Transport Synthesis
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the comprehensive understanding of proximal tubule (PT) transport mechanisms, specifically focusing on the basolateral exit pathways for sodium (Na+) and bicarbonate (HCO3-) reabsorption, and the key transporter responsible for this coupled process. (考察近曲小管转运机制的全面理解，特别是钠离子和碳酸氢根离子重吸收的基底外侧排出途径，以及负责此耦合过程的关键转运蛋白。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with complaints of nausea, vomiting, and generalized weakness for the past 2 days. He has a history of well-controlled type 1 diabetes mellitus, managed with subcutaneous insulin injections. He reports accidentally taking a double dose of his insulin yesterday morning. Physical examination reveals tachycardia (110 bpm), hypotension (90/60 mmHg), and signs of dehydration (dry mucous membranes, decreased skin turgor). Laboratory results show: Serum Na+ 138 mEq/L, K+ 4.8 mEq/L, Cl- 100 mEq/L, HCO3- 14 mEq/L (low), BUN 35 mg/dL, Creatinine 1.8 mg/dL (elevated), Glucose 45 mg/dL (low). Arterial blood gas analysis reveals pH 7.28, pCO2 25 mmHg, pO2 95 mmHg, HCO3- 14 mEq/L. Urine studies show: Urine Na+ 20 mEq/L, Urine K+ 30 mEq/L, Urine Cl- 15 mEq/L, Urine HCO3- 5 mEq/L, Urine Glucose 150 mg/dL, Urine pH 6.5, Fractional Excretion of Sodium (FeNa) 1.5% (low), Fractional Excretion of Potassium (FeK) 3%. The patient is diagnosed with diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA) complicated by acute kidney injury (AKI) likely secondary to dehydration and hypoperfusion. The metabolic acidosis is primarily due to ketoacid accumulation, but the renal handling of bicarbonate is also relevant. Which membrane transport protein is primarily responsible for the basolateral exit of both reabsorbed sodium and bicarbonate in the proximal convoluted tubule?

(A) Sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 (SGLT2)
(B) Sodium-phosphate cotransporter 2a (NaPi-IIa)
(C) Sodium-hydrogen exchanger 3 (NHE3)
(D) Basolateral electrogenic sodium-bicarbonate cotransporter (NBCe1)
(E) Carbonic anhydrase 4 (CA IV)

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Physiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient presents with DKA, characterized by hyperglycemia (initially, now corrected by insulin overdose), ketoacidosis (low HCO3-, low pH, low pCO2), and dehydration leading to pre-renal AKI (elevated BUN/Cr, low FeNa).
- The question specifically asks about the basolateral exit pathway for Na+ and HCO3- in the proximal convoluted tubule (PT).
- The vignette provides context (DKA, AKI) but the core question is purely physiological regarding PT transport.
- Key lab values: Low serum HCO3- (14 mEq/L) indicates metabolic acidosis. Urine HCO3- (5 mEq/L) is low, suggesting impaired renal bicarbonate reabsorption, which is expected in acidosis to conserve HCO3-. Urine glucose (150 mg/dL) indicates glucosuria due to SGLT2 saturation in DKA. Low FeNa (<1%) suggests pre-renal azotemia where the PT is avidly reabsorbing Na+ and water.

##### 2. Detailed Physiological Mechanism & Nephron Segment Biophysics (肾脏节段转运机制与生物物理推导)
- **Proximal Tubule (PT) Overview:** The PT is the primary site for reabsorption of filtered solutes and water (~65% of filtered load). It consists of three segments: S1, S2, and S3.
- **Bicarbonate Reabsorption:**
    - **Apical Entry:** Filtered HCO3- enters the PT cell primarily via the Na+-H+ exchanger 3 (NHE3) located on the apical membrane. NHE3 exchanges intracellular H+ for luminal Na+. The intracellular H+ combines with filtered HCO3- to form H2CO3, which is rapidly converted to CO2 and H2O by carbonic anhydrase 4 (CA IV) located in the apical cytoplasm.
    - **Intracellular Transport:** CO2 diffuses into the cell cytoplasm.
    - **Basolateral Exit:** CO2 diffuses out of the cell into the interstitium. In the interstitial fluid, CO2 combines with H2O to form H2CO3, catalyzed by carbonic anhydrase 2 (CA II) located on the basolateral membrane (or in the cytoplasm near the basolateral membrane). H2CO3 dissociates into H+ and HCO3-. The H+ is secreted into the interstitium via the H+-ATPase (apical) or H+/K+-ATPase (apical). The HCO3- is transported out of the cell across the basolateral membrane via the **basolateral electrogenic sodium-bicarbonate cotransporter (NBCe1)**. This transporter couples the movement of Na+ down its electrochemical gradient with the movement of HCO3- out of the cell.
- **Sodium Reabsorption:** Na+ enters the PT cell via NHE3 (apical) and also via Na+/H+ exchanger 4 (NHE4) in the early PT. It exits the cell basolaterally primarily via the Na+/K+-ATPase pump, which maintains the low intracellular Na+ concentration. The NBCe1 transporter also contributes to basolateral Na+ exit, coupled with HCO3- exit.
- **Key Transporters Mentioned:**
    - **SGLT2:** Apical transporter in S1 segment for glucose reabsorption (coupled with Na+).
    - **NaPi-IIa:** Apical transporter in S1/S2 segments for phosphate reabsorption (coupled with Na+).
    - **NHE3:** Apical transporter in S1/S2 segments for Na+ reabsorption (coupled with H+ secretion) and apical HCO3- entry.
    - **NBCe1:** Basolateral transporter responsible for the exit of both Na+ and HCO3- from the PT cell into the interstitium.
    - **CA IV:** Cytoplasmic enzyme in the PT cell, converts H2CO3 to CO2 and H2O (apical).

##### 3. Step-by-Step Clinical Deduction (临床逻辑逐步推理)
1. The question asks for the basolateral exit pathway for Na+ and HCO3- in the PT.
2. Recall the process of PT bicarbonate reabsorption: HCO3- enters the cell via NHE3 (apical), is converted to CO2 and H2O by CA IV (cytoplasmic), CO2 diffuses out, is converted back to HCO3- by CA II (basolateral), and HCO3- exits the cell.
3. Identify the transporter responsible for the basolateral exit of HCO3-. This is the NBCe1 transporter.
4. Note that NBCe1 is an electrogenic Na+-HCO3- cotransporter, meaning it also transports Na+ out of the cell basolaterally, contributing to the overall Na+ reabsorption and generating a lumen-negative potential.
5. Evaluate the options:
    - (A) SGLT2 is apical, for glucose. Incorrect.
    - (B) NaPi-IIa is apical, for phosphate. Incorrect.
    - (C) NHE3 is apical, for Na+ entry and H+ secretion (indirectly involved in HCO3- entry). Incorrect.
    - (D) NBCe1 is basolateral and transports both Na+ and HCO3- out. Correct.
    - (E) CA IV is cytoplasmic, catalyzes CO2/H2CO3 interconversion (apical side). Incorrect.

##### 4. Distractor Analysis (干扰项逐一深度剖析与鉴别)
- **(A) Sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 (SGLT2):** This is an apical transporter located primarily in the S1 segment of the PT. It is responsible for the reabsorption of glucose coupled with Na+ transport. It is not involved in the basolateral exit of bicarbonate. Incorrect.
- **(B) Sodium-phosphate cotransporter 2a (NaPi-IIa):** This is an apical transporter located in the S1 and S2 segments of the PT. It is responsible for the reabsorption of phosphate coupled with Na+ transport. It is not involved in the basolateral exit of bicarbonate. Incorrect.
- **(C) Sodium-hydrogen exchanger 3 (NHE3):** This is an apical transporter located in the S1 and S2 segments of the PT. It exchanges intracellular H+ for luminal Na+. This process is crucial for the initial uptake of filtered bicarbonate (HCO3- + H+ -> H2CO3) and also contributes to Na+ reabsorption. However, it is located on the apical membrane, not the basolateral membrane, and does not directly exit bicarbonate. Incorrect.
- **(D) Basolateral electrogenic sodium-bicarbonate cotransporter (NBCe1):** This transporter is located on the basolateral membrane of PT cells. It cotransports one Na+ ion and one HCO3- ion out of the cell into the interstitium. This is the primary mechanism for the basolateral exit of reabsorbed bicarbonate and contributes significantly to overall Na+ reabsorption. This matches the question's requirement. Correct.
- **(E) Carbonic anhydrase 4 (CA IV):** This enzyme is located in the apical cytoplasm of PT cells. It catalyzes the rapid interconversion of CO2 and H2O to H2CO3 and vice versa. It facilitates the hydration of CO2 (formed from H2CO3) to H+ and HCO3- at the apical membrane, which is essential for bicarbonate reabsorption, but it is an enzyme, not a membrane transport protein responsible for exiting HCO3- across the basolateral membrane. Incorrect.

##### 5. High-Yield Summary Table (高分必备核心对比表)
| Feature             | SGLT2 (Apical S1) | NaPi-IIa (Apical S1/S2) | NHE3 (Apical S1/S2) | NBCe1 (Basolateral) | CA IV (Apical Cytoplasm) |
|----------------------|-------------------|------------------------|--------------------|----------------------|--------------------------|
| Location            | Apical S1         | Apical S1/S2           | Apical S1/S2      | Basolateral          | Apical Cytoplasm         |
| Transported Species | Glucose + Na+     | Phosphate + Na+        | Na+ (in) + H+ (out) | Na+ + HCO3- (out)    | Catalyzes CO2 <-> H2CO3  |
| Role                | Glucose Reabsorption | Phosphate Reabsorption | Na+ Reabsorption, HCO3- Entry | HCO3- Exit, Na+ Reabsorption | Facilitates HCO3- Reabsorption |

#### Educational Objective / 核心考点总结:
The basolateral exit of reabsorbed bicarbonate from the proximal tubule cell into the peritubular interstitium is primarily mediated by the electrogenic sodium-bicarbonate cotransporter (NBCe1). This transporter couples the movement of Na+ and HCO3- out of the cell, contributing to both sodium reabsorption and bicarbonate reclamation. Understanding the location and function of NBCe1 is crucial for comprehending the overall mechanism of proximal tubule bicarbonate handling.
